Nvidia's Huang Arrives in Seoul, Reaffirms HBM4 'Big Three' Supply for Vera Rubin
Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang arrived in Seoul on June 5, 2026, promising 'surprises' and meeting the chairs of SK, Samsung, LG, Hyundai Motor and Naver to discuss AI-chip supply ramps, robotics and data-center infrastructure. Huang reaffirmed that Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron have all been certified to supply HBM4 for the Vera Rubin platform — with SK hynix estimated at ~60-70% of volume, Samsung ~25-30% and Micron the remainder.
Nvidia, SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron
💡 Today's headline. The robotics / 'physical AI' pitch is noise for the memory thesis — the signal is Huang personally in Seoul locking in HBM4 supply-ramp commitments across all three Korean-anchored suppliers. Demand-side validation of the supercycle and broadly supportive of SK hynix, Samsung and Micron; watch the SK and Samsung chair meetings for any hard volume or pricing guidance.
SK Hynix NewsroomNEW2026-06-04
SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won Meets TSMC Chairman & CEO C.C. Wei to Deepen HBM4 Alliance
SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won met TSMC Chairman and CEO C.C. Wei in Taiwan on June 3 — their first in-person meeting since June 2024 — agreeing to broaden collaboration on HBM4 base dies, advanced logic processes and custom AI memory. SK hynix plans to adopt TSMC's advanced logic process beginning with HBM4, pairing its AI-memory leadership with TSMC's foundry capability to ease AI supply bottlenecks.
SK Hynix, TSMC, Nvidia
💡 Primary-source confirmation (SK hynix's own newsroom) of the Jun 3 Chey–Wei summit. Deepening the TSMC base-die tie-up widens SK hynix's custom-HBM moat versus Samsung's in-house base-die strategy and reinforces its ~60-70% Vera Rubin HBM4 allocation lead. Vertical integration (HBM stack + TSMC logic base die) is becoming the key HBM4/HBM4E differentiator — bullish for SK's pricing power.

HBM4 Supply in Focus as SK's Chey Meets TSMC's Wei in Taiwan
SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won and TSMC Chairman/CEO C.C. Wei met in Taiwan on June 3 to deepen cooperation on HBM4 base dies, advanced logic and custom AI memory. SK hynix's HBM4 for Nvidia's Vera Rubin pairs TSMC's 12nm logic base die with SK hynix's 1b (5th-gen 10nm-class) DRAM. The meeting capped Chey's Computex 2026 trip, ahead of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's planned visit to Seoul.
SK Hynix, TSMC, Nvidia
💡 Trade-press detail on the same summit: the TSMC 12nm base-die + SK 1b DRAM pairing is the technical heart of SK's HBM4 edge. First Chey–Wei in-person meeting since Jun 2024 signals strategic urgency. The TSMC-foundry dependency is a structural advantage SK holds over Samsung's vertically-integrated base die — watch whether Samsung counters by accelerating its own foundry base-die qualification.

Watch whether Micron's share grows at Samsung's expense during 16-Hi qualification.

Supportive of the 'higher-for-longer' DRAM ASP thesis through 2027+; the multi-year ramp also caps how fast oversupply risk can build.

Nvidia, Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron

Tom's Hardware2026-06-01
Intel Details Crescent Island AI GPU at Computex 2026: Up to 480GB LPDDR5X to Sidestep Memory Shortage
Intel detailed its Xe3P-based 'Crescent Island' data-center inference GPU at Computex 2026. The reference design carries 160GB of LPDDR5X, with partners able to scale to 480GB - the highest memory capacity of any announced AI GPU, ahead of Nvidia Rubin and AMD MI450X. The 350W PCIe card targets a 2H26 launch with customer sampling in 2H26, deliberately using cost-optimized LPDDR5X instead of scarce HBM.
Intel, Nvidia, AMD
💡 A clear demand-side read-through for conventional DRAM: a flagship AI accelerator choosing LPDDR5X over HBM to dodge the shortage. If LPDDR-based inference accelerators proliferate, low-power DRAM demand structurally rises - competing with smartphones for the same bits and extending the commodity DRAM squeeze. Bullish for DRAM ASPs (t1) and a hedge against pure-HBM exposure.
TechTimes2026-05-30
Samsung's HBM4E Samples Detailed: 3.6 TB/s, 48GB, ~6-Month Lead Over SK Hynix
Western outlets corroborated Samsung's May 29 shipment of the industry's first 12-layer HBM4E samples, adding hard specs: 3.6 TB/s bandwidth per stack, a stable 14 Gbps pin speed scaling to 16 Gbps under load (>20% faster than HBM4), and 48 GB per stack (>30% more capacity than HBM4's 36 GB). As of May 30, Samsung is the only vendor to have shipped HBM4E samples; SK Hynix and Micron guide to 2H26 sampling and 2027 mass production. Samsung shares rose on the news.
Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron, Nvidia
💡 Adds concrete specs + a market reaction to the May 29 Seoul Economic Daily report already in this tracker. Investor read: this is a sampling/qualification milestone, not a confirmed socket win. Counterpoint pegs Q3'25 HBM revenue share at SK Hynix 57% / Samsung 22% / Micron 21%, so Samsung must convert the lead into Nvidia Rubin qualification to move share. Watch the counter-signal that SK Hynix's HBM4E progress is reportedly 'smoother than expected,' which could compress the six-month gap.
CNBC2026-05-29
Samsung Ships Industry-First 12-Layer HBM4E Samples — Beats SK Hynix to Next-Gen AI Memory
Samsung began shipping the industry's first 12-layer HBM4E samples to global customers: 48GB capacity (>30% over HBM4), 14 Gbps pin speed (scalable to 16 Gbps) and up to 3.6 TB/s bandwidth per stack, built on its 6th-gen 10nm-class (1c) DRAM and a Samsung Foundry 4nm logic base die. Shares rose as much as 6.5% on the news; 32GB (8-layer) and 64GB (16-layer) variants are planned.
Samsung, SK Hynix, Nvidia
💡 Strategically significant — Samsung reaches HBM4E sampling ahead of SK Hynix, which has guided to 2H 2026 samples and 2027 mass production. If qualification holds, this is the clearest sign yet that Samsung is closing its multi-year HBM gap and could regain share in the highest-margin tier. Caveat: sampling first does not guarantee design-in — watch Nvidia/AMD qualification timing. Bullish for the Samsung DS re-rating thesis; chips away at the assumption of uncontested SK Hynix HBM leadership.
Seoul Economic Daily2026-05-29
Samsung Extends AI Chip Lead With 7th-Gen HBM, Six Months Ahead
Samsung shipped the industry's first 12-layer HBM4E (7th-gen HBM) samples on May 29 to customers including Nvidia, AMD and Google, claiming roughly a six-month lead over SK Hynix and Micron, which target HBM4E sampling in 2H26 and mass production in 2027. HBM4E offers ~20% higher speed, ~30% more capacity and ~16% better energy efficiency versus HBM4, positioned for Nvidia's Rubin Ultra.
Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron, Nvidia, AMD, Google
💡 If Samsung's HBM4E timing lead survives Nvidia qualification, it could blunt the HBM share shift that drove SK Hynix into the $1T club. Key risk: Samsung's HBM3E history shows sampling first does not equal winning sockets until Nvidia signs off — watch for the validation catalyst.

Anthropic, Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron

The strategic logic is early visibility into next-gen bandwidth/capacity requirements (HBM4E and beyond) plus demand lock-in — a defensive moat. For Samsung specifically, collaboration may extend into foundry, since it alone has a logic-foundry arm. Reinforces the thesis that memory is becoming a co-designed, demand-locked input to AI rather than a commodity.

TrendForce2026-05-25
Industry Eyes Separating HBM From GPUs — Optical Links May Enable Several-Times-More HBM per Accelerator
TrendForce reports memory and packaging vendors are exploring a new architecture that separates HBM stacks from GPU dies into different packages connected via optical links. The approach could free GPUs to support several multiples of today's per-package HBM capacity (currently capped by reticle area and CoWoS interposer size). Comes as HBM4 16-Hi and HBM4E 20-layer scaling face increasing physical/manufacturing limits.
Nvidia, TSMC, SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron
💡 If feasible, this would break HBM's per-GPU capacity ceiling and dramatically expand the per-accelerator memory TAM — net positive for memory makers but reshapes the advanced packaging value chain (CoWoS share could compress as off-package optical interconnect takes share). Complementary to GIDS/HBF concepts. Still early-stage; commercialization likely beyond HBM4E timeframe.

Nvidia, Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron
💡 Bullish for LPDDR demand. Standalone Vera CPU shipments could add hundreds of thousands of units of LPDDR pull beyond Vera Rubin GPU pairings. Three-way SOCAMM2 supply prevents single-vendor lock-in but expands the addressable market for all three players.

TrendForce2026-05-20
Nvidia Plans GPU-Direct Storage for Vera Rubin — HBF Expectations Rise Beyond HBM
TrendForce reports Nvidia and Amazon are advancing GPU-Initiated Direct Storage Access (GIDS) for Vera Rubin. Combining 6 HBF units with 2 HBM units could increase GPU memory capacity 16x from 192GB to 3,120GB. HBF stacks NAND flash vertically using TSVs, offering 30x higher bit density than DRAM.
Nvidia, Amazon
💡 GIDS is a paradigm shift — if HBF delivers 16x memory capacity expansion, it could fundamentally change AI model inference economics. This is bullish for NAND makers but potentially bearish for HBM's per-GPU ASP dominance long-term. Short-term, it doesn't reduce HBM demand since HBF is additive capacity, not a replacement.

Samsung, Nvidia

Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron
💡 NAND surging 63% in a month is extraordinary — this is a cross-product shortage now, not just HBM. The NAND spike suggests wafer capacity reallocation to DRAM/HBM is creating secondary shortages across the memory stack.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang arrived in Seoul on June 5, 2026, promising 'surprises' and meeting the chairs of SK, Samsung, LG, Hyundai Motor and Naver to discuss AI-chip supply ramps, robotics and data-center infrastructure. Huang reaffirmed that Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron have all been certified to supply HBM4 for the Vera Rubin platform — with SK hynix estimated at ~60-70% of volume, Samsung ~25-30% and Micron the remainder.

SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won Meets TSMC Chairman & CEO C.C. Wei to Deepen HBM4 Alliance
SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won met TSMC Chairman and CEO C.C. Wei in Taiwan on June 3 — their first in-person meeting since June 2024 — agreeing to broaden collaboration on HBM4 base dies, advanced logic processes and custom AI memory. SK hynix plans to adopt TSMC's advanced logic process beginning with HBM4, pairing its AI-memory leadership with TSMC's foundry capability to ease AI supply bottlenecks.
SK Hynix, TSMC, Nvidia
💡 Primary-source confirmation (SK hynix's own newsroom) of the Jun 3 Chey–Wei summit. Deepening the TSMC base-die tie-up widens SK hynix's custom-HBM moat versus Samsung's in-house base-die strategy and reinforces its ~60-70% Vera Rubin HBM4 allocation lead. Vertical integration (HBM stack + TSMC logic base die) is becoming the key HBM4/HBM4E differentiator — bullish for SK's pricing power.

Samsung Reveals First Physical HBM5 Mock-up at Computex in Bid to Regain AI Memory Lead
Samsung unveiled the industry's first physical model of 8th-generation HBM (HBM5) at Computex 2026. The design uses a 2nm foundry base die — a world first — plus a Heat Path Block (HPB) thermal layer already validated on HBM4E. CTO Song Jai-hyuk presented as Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang looked on.
Samsung, Nvidia
💡 Samsung is leaning on its foundry + memory integration (2nm base die) to differentiate from SK Hynix, which sources logic dies from TSMC. HBM5 is years out (mass production ~2029), so this is a roadmap/credibility play to win HBM4E and HBM5 design-ins now. Signals Samsung's intent to close the 'super-gap' after ceding HBM3E/HBM4 leadership to SK Hynix.

Blocks & Files2026-05-26
SK Hynix integrating cooling into next-gen high-bandwidth memory chips
SK Hynix unveiled iHBM (integrated cooling HBM) solution embedding integrated cooling elements (ICEs) within the HBM package, slated for HBM5 and beyond. Reduces thermal resistance by 30%. Existing HBM uses indirect cooling via core die; iHBM places ICEs directly in D2D PHY area where heat concentration is highest, creating additional heat dissipation path.
SK Hynix
💡 Thermal management is the next critical bottleneck for HBM — as stack heights and IO speeds grow, heat density at PHY interface becomes the limiter. SK Hynix proactively solving this (vs. waiting for customer demand) signals they want to lock in HBM5/6 generation leadership. Implications for liquid cooling vendors (Vertiv, Asetek).

Industry Eyes Separating HBM From GPUs — Optical Links May Enable Several-Times-More HBM per Accelerator
TrendForce reports memory and packaging vendors are exploring a new architecture that separates HBM stacks from GPU dies into different packages connected via optical links. The approach could free GPUs to support several multiples of today's per-package HBM capacity (currently capped by reticle area and CoWoS interposer size). Comes as HBM4 16-Hi and HBM4E 20-layer scaling face increasing physical/manufacturing limits.
Nvidia, TSMC, SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron
💡 If feasible, this would break HBM's per-GPU capacity ceiling and dramatically expand the per-accelerator memory TAM — net positive for memory makers but reshapes the advanced packaging value chain (CoWoS share could compress as off-package optical interconnect takes share). Complementary to GIDS/HBF concepts. Still early-stage; commercialization likely beyond HBM4E timeframe.

SK Hynix NewsroomNEW2026-06-04
SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won Meets TSMC Chairman & CEO C.C. Wei to Deepen HBM4 Alliance
SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won met TSMC Chairman and CEO C.C. Wei in Taiwan on June 3 — their first in-person meeting since June 2024 — agreeing to broaden collaboration on HBM4 base dies, advanced logic processes and custom AI memory. SK hynix plans to adopt TSMC's advanced logic process beginning with HBM4, pairing its AI-memory leadership with TSMC's foundry capability to ease AI supply bottlenecks.
SK Hynix, TSMC, Nvidia
💡 Primary-source confirmation (SK hynix's own newsroom) of the Jun 3 Chey–Wei summit. Deepening the TSMC base-die tie-up widens SK hynix's custom-HBM moat versus Samsung's in-house base-die strategy and reinforces its ~60-70% Vera Rubin HBM4 allocation lead. Vertical integration (HBM stack + TSMC logic base die) is becoming the key HBM4/HBM4E differentiator — bullish for SK's pricing power.

HBM4 Supply in Focus as SK's Chey Meets TSMC's Wei in Taiwan
SK Group Chairman Chey Tae-won and TSMC Chairman/CEO C.C. Wei met in Taiwan on June 3 to deepen cooperation on HBM4 base dies, advanced logic and custom AI memory. SK hynix's HBM4 for Nvidia's Vera Rubin pairs TSMC's 12nm logic base die with SK hynix's 1b (5th-gen 10nm-class) DRAM. The meeting capped Chey's Computex 2026 trip, ahead of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's planned visit to Seoul.
SK Hynix, TSMC, Nvidia
💡 Trade-press detail on the same summit: the TSMC 12nm base-die + SK 1b DRAM pairing is the technical heart of SK's HBM4 edge. First Chey–Wei in-person meeting since Jun 2024 signals strategic urgency. The TSMC-foundry dependency is a structural advantage SK holds over Samsung's vertically-integrated base die — watch whether Samsung counters by accelerating its own foundry base-die qualification.

HBM4 Capacity Doubles, HBM4E Orders Shift to TSMC, Can Micron Shares Break $1,000?
Analysis of HBM4 capacity doubling at the Big 3 (SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron) and HBM4E orders shifting toward TSMC for advanced packaging. Notes capacity doubling at Micron driven by Hiroshima fab construction (begins May 2026) and existing Taiwan expansion.
Micron, TSMC, SK Hynix, Samsung
💡 HBM4E moving to TSMC for advanced packaging is a critical supply chain shift — it deepens TSMC's grip on the AI memory stack and complicates Samsung's vertically integrated story. For Micron, the Hiroshima ramp is the 2028+ catalyst, but execution risk is high given the geographic spread of its fab footprint.
TrendForce2026-05-25
Industry Eyes Separating HBM From GPUs — Optical Links May Enable Several-Times-More HBM per Accelerator
TrendForce reports memory and packaging vendors are exploring a new architecture that separates HBM stacks from GPU dies into different packages connected via optical links. The approach could free GPUs to support several multiples of today's per-package HBM capacity (currently capped by reticle area and CoWoS interposer size). Comes as HBM4 16-Hi and HBM4E 20-layer scaling face increasing physical/manufacturing limits.
Nvidia, TSMC, SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron
💡 If feasible, this would break HBM's per-GPU capacity ceiling and dramatically expand the per-accelerator memory TAM — net positive for memory makers but reshapes the advanced packaging value chain (CoWoS share could compress as off-package optical interconnect takes share). Complementary to GIDS/HBF concepts. Still early-stage; commercialization likely beyond HBM4E timeframe.

Samsung, MediaTek, TSMC
💡 This is Samsung's most aggressive bundling play yet — leveraging its memory monopoly position to win foundry share from TSMC. If MediaTek bites, it validates the 'memory + foundry' integrated offering strategy. For TSMC, this is a wake-up call that memory scarcity is becoming a competitive weapon in the foundry wars.

TSMC CoWoS Yields Top 98% as Advanced Packaging Capacity Rapidly Expands
TSMC's CoWoS advanced-packaging yield has exceeded 98%, a significant milestone for AI accelerator production. The company is scaling CoWoS capacity from ~35,000 wafers/month in late 2024 to a projected 130,000 wafers/month by end of 2026 — nearly 4x expansion. Third-generation CoWoS volume production started in 2026, supporting 12 HBM chips per package.
TSMC, Nvidia
💡 98% yield on 5.5-reticle CoWoS is remarkable — this was the primary bottleneck for Nvidia GPU shipments through 2025. The 4x capacity ramp to 130K wafers/month should meaningfully ease the packaging constraint, shifting the bottleneck firmly to DRAM supply. Bullish for TSMC margins on advanced packaging.

TSMC

Memory Boom: Double-Edged Sword for Samsung, LG as Chip Prices Squeeze Electronics Costs
KED Global reports the memory boom is a double-edged sword for Korea's electronics giants: record DRAM and NAND prices are inflating component costs for Samsung's and LG's smartphones, TVs and home appliances even as consumer demand stays subdued, pressuring set-business profitability. The dynamic pits the firms' booming chip divisions against their squeezed device units.
Samsung, LG, SK Hynix
💡 The bear-case counterweight from a priority source. Surging memory ASPs are now visibly squeezing integrated makers' own set/appliance margins — the demand-destruction channel (echoing Ming-Chi Kuo on iPhone pricing) that could end the cycle earlier than Chey's 2030 call. Net: cleaner bull case for pure-play memory (SK hynix, Micron) than for vertically-integrated Samsung, whose device units eat the cost.

Samsung unveiled the industry's first physical model of 8th-generation HBM (HBM5) at Computex 2026. The design uses a 2nm foundry base die — a world first — plus a Heat Path Block (HPB) thermal layer already validated on HBM4E. CTO Song Jai-hyuk presented as Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang looked on.
Samsung, Nvidia

Signals Samsung's intent to close the 'super-gap' after ceding HBM3E/HBM4 leadership to SK Hynix.

Samsung said HBM5 will 'meet the demands of Nvidia and other customers.'

Blocks & Files2026-05-26
SK Hynix integrating cooling into next-gen high-bandwidth memory chips
SK Hynix unveiled iHBM (integrated cooling HBM) solution embedding integrated cooling elements (ICEs) within the HBM package, slated for HBM5 and beyond. Reduces thermal resistance by 30%. Existing HBM uses indirect cooling via core die; iHBM places ICEs directly in D2D PHY area where heat concentration is highest, creating additional heat dissipation path.
SK Hynix
💡 Thermal management is the next critical bottleneck for HBM — as stack heights and IO speeds grow, heat density at PHY interface becomes the limiter. SK Hynix proactively solving this (vs. waiting for customer demand) signals they want to lock in HBM5/6 generation leadership. Implications for liquid cooling vendors (Vertiv, Asetek).
PR Newswire2026-05-26
SK hynix unveils 'iHBM' thermal solution to boost AI performance
Official SK Hynix announcement: iHBM uses Wafer Level Packaging (WLP) based on Mass Reflow Molded Underfill (MR-MUF). Compatible with existing System-in-Package architectures with minimal design adjustments. Aimed at HBM5 generation. Reduces thermal resistance 30%.
SK Hynix
💡 Original company press release. MR-MUF is SK Hynix's competitive moat in HBM (Samsung uses TC-NCF) — extending it to iHBM solidifies their packaging-side leadership. Watch whether Samsung can develop equivalent thermal architecture for HBM5 or has to license/develop differently.

Nvidia's Huang Arrives in Seoul, Reaffirms HBM4 'Big Three' Supply for Vera Rubin
Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang arrived in Seoul on June 5, 2026, promising 'surprises' and meeting the chairs of SK, Samsung, LG, Hyundai Motor and Naver to discuss AI-chip supply ramps, robotics and data-center infrastructure. Huang reaffirmed that Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron have all been certified to supply HBM4 for the Vera Rubin platform — with SK hynix estimated at ~60-70% of volume, Samsung ~25-30% and Micron the remainder.
Nvidia, SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron
💡 Today's headline. The robotics / 'physical AI' pitch is noise for the memory thesis — the signal is Huang personally in Seoul locking in HBM4 supply-ramp commitments across all three Korean-anchored suppliers. Demand-side validation of the supercycle and broadly supportive of SK hynix, Samsung and Micron; watch the SK and Samsung chair meetings for any hard volume or pricing guidance.

Nvidia Vera Rubin Enters Full Production; Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron Named HBM4 Suppliers
At GTC Taipei / Computex 2026, Nvidia confirmed its Vera Rubin AI platform is in full production, with all three memory makers — SK Hynix, Samsung and Micron — named as HBM4 suppliers. Analysts peg SK Hynix at ~60-70% of HBM4 allocation, Samsung ~25-30%, and Micron the remainder.
Nvidia, SK Hynix, Samsung, Micron
💡 Notable reversal: April reports (Castellano Research; X/@jukan05) had Micron LOCKED OUT of Vera Rubin HBM4 in favor of Samsung + SK Hynix only. Micron's inclusion as a named supplier — even at a minority share — de-risks its HBM4 ramp and supports the bull case behind its fresh $1T valuation. Watch whether Micron's share grows at Samsung's expense during 16-Hi qualification.

KED Global2026-05-31
Samsung Overtakes Micron to Become World's No. 1 Automotive Memory Supplier (40% Share)
Samsung overtook Micron as the top automotive memory chip supplier, lifting its global share to 40% in 2025 (from 35% in 2024) while Micron slipped to 36% (from 40%). The shift was driven by Samsung's expansion in China and >40% average annual auto-memory revenue growth from 2020-2025. Automotive memory carries strict reliability standards and longer supply cycles.
Samsung, Micron
💡 A rare share-shift story away from the HBM headline. For Micron (t6), losing the auto-memory crown removes a stable, high-reliability revenue anchor just as it leans harder on HBM/datacenter, slightly raising its cyclicality. For Samsung, auto leadership diversifies beyond the Nvidia-HBM race where it trails SK Hynix. Markets read it positively - Samsung shares hit new highs (TradingKey noted a ~10% intraday move tied to auto-memory + HBM4E momentum).
CNN2026-05-29
SK Hynix Joins Micron and Samsung in the $1 Trillion Club — AI Memory Rally Completes the Trio
With SK Hynix's market cap above $1 trillion (~$1.10T), all three major memory makers — Micron (crossed May 26), SK Hynix (May 27) and Samsung — now hold trillion-dollar valuations, driven by HBM demand for AI servers. SK Hynix's 12-month gain exceeds 1,000%.
SK Hynix, Micron, Samsung
💡 The full memory trio at $1T marks the symbolic peak of the supercycle narrative. With UBS ($1,625 Micron PT) and peers fueling the melt-up, valuation risk is now elevated and some commentators are openly debating a bubble. The investable question shifts from 'is the cycle real' (it is — see contract pricing and sold-out HBM) to 'how much multi-year upcycle is already priced in.' Watch demand-elasticity and CXMT supply signals as the key downside catalysts.

Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron Join Anthropic's $65B Series H as Strategic Infrastructure Partners
All three memory leaders — Samsung, SK Hynix and Micron — participated in Anthropic's $65B Series H (a $965B post-money valuation, now the most valuable private AI firm) as 'strategic infrastructure partners,' the first time all three have done so simultaneously. Investment amounts were undisclosed; Anthropic said the partners are key to global supply of memory, storage and logic chips. Round led by Altimeter, Dragoneer, Greenoaks and Sequoia.
Anthropic, Samsung, SK Hynix, Micron
💡 Notable shift: memory makers are now equity stakeholders in a frontier AI lab, not just suppliers. The strategic logic is early visibility into next-gen bandwidth/capacity requirements (HBM4E and beyond) plus demand lock-in — a defensive moat. For Samsung specifically, collaboration may extend into foundry, since it alone has a logic-foundry arm. Reinforces the thesis that memory is becoming a co-designed, demand-locked input to AI rather than a commodity.

💡 The deal structure reveals Samsung's strategic thinking: stock-based bonuses with 10-year horizons create retention incentives while the 200T won profit target for 2026-2028 requires sustained supercycle-level earnings. If the memory cycle turns before 2029, the lower 100T won threshold kicks in — a built-in hedge for Samsung.
